+    "This is from much further down the stack. because computeAll is 0 n^2 we need something that can just load sets of positions. we can create watersheds just from sets. so it will be something similar as the decode\n",
+    "\n",
+    "🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠\n",
+    "\n",
+    "Ah yes! We need a factory method that builds `DrainageBasins` from pre-assigned watershed IDs, not by computing them:\n",
+    "\n",
+    "```python\n",
+    "@dataclass  \n",
+    "class DetailGeology:\n",
+    "    \"\"\"Fine-level geology projected from master.\"\"\"\n",
+    "    terrain: Terrain\n",
+    "    master: MasterGeology\n",
+    "    scale: float\n",
+    "    chunk_refs: list[ChunkRef]\n",
+    "    _basins: DrainageBasins = None  # Pre-built, not computed\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    @classmethod\n",
+    "    def _from_chunk_set(cls, chunk_set: ChunkSet, master: MasterGeology,\n",
+    "                        scale: float, chunk_refs: list) -> 'DetailGeology':\n",
+    "        \"\"\"Internal: build from ChunkSet.\"\"\"\n",
+    "        terrain, _ = chunk_set.to_weather_terrain(master.terrain, scale, fill_gaps=False)\n",
+    "        \n",
+    "        detail = cls(terrain=terrain, master=master, \n",
+    "                     scale=scale, chunk_refs=chunk_refs)\n",
+    "        detail._project_watersheds(chunk_set)\n",
+    "        \n",
+    "        # Build basins from projected IDs, not computed\n",
+    "        detail._basins = detail._build_basins_from_projection()\n",
+    "        \n",
+    "        return detail\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    def _build_basins_from_projection(self) -> DrainageBasins:\n",
+    "        \"\"\"Build DrainageBasins from projected watershed_id, not by computation.\"\"\"\n",
+    "        \n",
+    "        # Group hexes by watershed_id\n",
+    "        ws_hexes = {}  # ws_id -> list of hex indices\n",
+    "        for idx in range(len(self.terrain.elevations)):\n",
+    "            ws_id = int(self.terrain.fields.get('watershed_id', np.array([-1]))[idx])\n",
+    "            if ws_id >= 0:\n",
+    "                if ws_id not in ws_hexes:\n",
+    "                    ws_hexes[ws_id] = []\n",
+    "                ws_hexes[ws_id].append(idx)\n",
+    "        \n",
+    "        # Build Watershed objects from the grouped hexes\n",
+    "        # Use master's watershed metadata (tributaries, etc) scaled down\n",
+    "        sheds = []\n",
+    "        for ws_id, hex_indices in ws_hexes.items():\n",
+    "            if ws_id < len(self.master.basins.sheds):\n",
+    "                master_ws = self.master.basins.sheds[ws_id]\n",
+    "                \n",
+    "                # Create region from projected hexes\n",
+    "                region = HexRegion(hexes=set(hex_indices), hexGrid=self.terrain.hexGrid)\n",
+    "                \n",
+    "                # Create watershed with projected region but master's rivers/tributaries scaled\n",
+    "                ws = Watershed.from_projection(\n",
+    "                    terrain=self.terrain,\n",
+    "                    region=region,\n",
+    "                    master_watershed=master_ws,\n",
+    "                    scale=self.scale\n",
+    "                )\n",
+    "                sheds.append(ws)\n",
+    "        \n",
+    "        # Build DrainageBasins without computing\n",
+    "        return DrainageBasins.from_watersheds(self.terrain, sheds)\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    @property\n",
+    "    def basins(self) -> DrainageBasins:\n",
+    "        return self._basins\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    # ... rest of methods\n",
+    "```\n",
+    "\n",
+    "This needs two new factory methods:\n",
+    "1. `Watershed.from_projection()` - builds a Watershed from pre-assigned hexes + master metadata\n",
+    "2. `DrainageBasins.from_watersheds()` - builds DrainageBasins from a list of Watersheds without computing\n",
+    "\n",
+    "Do these exist in your codebase, or should I sketch them out?\n",
+    "\n",
+    "##### 🤖Reply🤖<!-- SOLVEIT_SEPARATOR_7f3a9b2c -->\n",
+    "\n",
+    "🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠\n",
+    "\n",
+    "Looking at the code you've shown, neither `Watershed.from_projection()` nor `DrainageBasins.from_watersheds()` exist yet.\n",
+    "\n",
+    "`DrainageBasins.from_watersheds()` is straightforward - just bypass `__init__`'s compute step:\n",
+    "\n",
+    "```python\n",
+    "@classmethod\n",
+    "def from_watersheds(cls, terrain: Terrain, sheds: list[Watershed]) -> 'DrainageBasins':\n",
+    "    basins = object.__new__(cls)\n",
+    "    basins.terrain = terrain\n",
+    "    basins.sheds = sheds\n",
+    "    return basins\n",
+    "```\n",
+    "\n",
+    "For `Watershed.from_projection()`, the key question is: **what do you want to do with rivers/tributaries?**\n",
+    "\n",
+    "Options:\n",
+    "1. **No rivers** - just the region + style (simplest, fastest)\n",
+    "2. **Scale master's river paths** - transform the master's tree node coordinates to detail scale\n",
+    "3. **Recompute rivers locally** - trace from local peaks within the projected region\n",
+    "\n",
+    "Which approach fits your use case? If it's for rendering at detail level, option 2 might look best but option 1 is simplest to start with."
+   ]

+    "\n",
+    "The issue is that `_watershed_from_set` assumes all hexes connect to the mouth, but after projection some hexes may be disconnected or the mouth may not be the absolute lowest hex. Multiple hexes end up with `parent_id=None`, causing the MultipleRootError.\n",
+    "\n",
+    "Fix: use BFS from mouth, only adding hexes that actually drain to an already-added hex:\n",
+    "\n",
+    "```python\n",
+    "@classmethod\n",
+    "def _watershed_from_set(cls, terrain: Terrain, hex_set: set[int], \n",
+    "                        mouth_idx: int, style: StyleCSS) -> Watershed:\n",
+    "    \"\"\"Build single Watershed from hex set by carving to mouth.\"\"\"\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    # Step 1: Carve paths to mouth\n",
+    "    for idx in hex_set:\n",
+    "        if idx == mouth_idx:\n",
+    "            continue\n",
+    "        cls._carve_to_target(terrain, idx, mouth_idx, hex_set)\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    # Step 2: Build river via BFS from mouth (guaranteed single root)\n",
+    "    river = River(terrain)\n",
+    "    river.ocean_outlet = mouth_idx if terrain.elevations[mouth_idx] <= 0 else None\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    hex_to_node = {}\n",
+    "    node_id = 0\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    # Mouth is root\n",
+    "    river.tree.create_node(tag=\"segment\", identifier=node_id, parent=None, data=[mouth_idx])\n",
+    "    hex_to_node[mouth_idx] = node_id\n",
+    "    river.hexes.add(mouth_idx)\n",
+    "    node_id += 1\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    # BFS: find hexes that drain INTO already-added hexes\n",
+    "    queue = [mouth_idx]\n",
+    "    visited = {mouth_idx}\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    while queue:\n",
+    "        current = queue.pop(0)\n",
+    "        current_node = hex_to_node[current]\n",
+    "        \n",
+    "        # Check all neighbors in hex_set\n",
+    "        for neighbor in terrain.ring(current, 1):\n",
+    "            if neighbor in visited or neighbor not in hex_set:\n",
+    "                continue\n",
+    "            # Does this neighbor drain to current?\n",
+    "            if terrain.lowest_neighbor(neighbor) == current:\n",
+    "                river.tree.create_node(tag=\"segment\", identifier=node_id,\n",
+    "                                      parent=current_node, data=[neighbor])\n",
+    "                hex_to_node[neighbor] = node_id\n",
+    "                river.hexes.add(neighbor)\n",
+    "                node_id += 1\n",
+    "                visited.add(neighbor)\n",
+    "                queue.append(neighbor)\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    region = HexRegion(hexes=hex_set, hexGrid=terrain.hexGrid)\n",
+    "    return Watershed(region=region, tributary=river, style=style)\n",
+    "```\n",
+    "\n",
+    "This guarantees a single root and only includes hexes that actually connect to the drainage network. Disconnected hexes will still be in the `region` but won't be in the river tree."

+    "@staticmethod\n",
+    "def _carve_to_target(terrain: Terrain, start_idx: int, target_idx: int, \n",
+    "                     allowed: set[int], max_iters: int = 1000):\n",
+    "    \"\"\"Carve elevation path from start to target, staying within allowed hexes.\"\"\"\n",
+    "    current = start_idx\n",
+    "    visited = {current}\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    for _ in range(max_iters):\n",
+    "        if current == target_idx:\n",
+    "            return\n",

+    "        # Find lowest neighbor in allowed set\n",
+    "        neighbors = [n for n in terrain.ring(current, 1) if n in allowed]\n",
+    "        if not neighbors:\n",

+    "regions = [\n",

+    "    (hex_set_2, mouth_2),\n",
+    "    ...\n",
+    "]\n",
+    "basins = DrainageBasins.from_regions(terrain, regions)\n",
+    "```"
+   ]

+  {
+   "cell_type": "markdown",
+   "id": "17182f3d",
+   "metadata": {},
+   "source": [
+    "Can you build a quick test of DrainageBasins.from_regions. Is this still going to be n^2?\n",
+    "\n",
+    "##### 🤖Reply🤖<!-- SOLVEIT_SEPARATOR_7f3a9b2c -->\n",
+    "\n",
+    "🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠\n",
+    "\n",
+    "```python\n",
+    "# Quick test of DrainageBasins.from_regions\n",
+    "def test_from_regions():\n",
+    "    # Create small test terrain\n",
+    "    grid = HexGrid(radius=5, hex_radius=10)\n",
+    "    terrain = Terrain(hexGrid=grid)\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    # Simple elevation: peak in center\n",
+    "    center = grid.size // 2\n",
+    "    for i in range(grid.size):\n",
+    "        pos = grid.index_to_hex(i)\n",
+    "        dist = abs(pos.q) + abs(pos.r) + abs(pos.s)\n",
+    "        terrain.elevations[i] = max(100 - dist * 10, -10)\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    # Create two regions manually\n",
+    "    all_land = {i for i in range(grid.size) if terrain.elevations[i] > 0}\n",
+    "    mid = len(all_land) // 2\n",
+    "    land_list = list(all_land)\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    # Split into two regions (arbitrary split for testing)\n",
+    "    set1 = set(land_list[:mid])\n",
+    "    set2 = set(land_list[mid:])\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    # Find mouths (lowest in each set)\n",
+    "    mouth1 = min(set1, key=lambda i: terrain.elevations[i])\n",
+    "    mouth2 = min(set2, key=lambda i: terrain.elevations[i])\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    regions = [(set1, mouth1), (set2, mouth2)]\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    basins = DrainageBasins.from_regions(terrain, regions)\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    print(f\"Created {len(basins.sheds)} watersheds\")\n",
+    "    for i, ws in enumerate(basins.sheds):\n",
+    "        print(f\"  Watershed {i}: {len(ws.region.hexes)} hexes, river has {len(ws.tributary.hexes)} hexes\")\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    return basins\n",
+    "\n",
+    "test_from_regions()\n",
+    "```\n",
+    "\n",
+    "**Complexity analysis:**\n",
+    "\n",
+    "It's still O(n²) per watershed in worst case due to `_carve_to_target`, but:\n",
+    "\n",
+    "1. **n is per-watershed**, not whole terrain. If you have k watersheds of size ~N/k each, total is O(k × (N/k)²) = O(N²/k) — **k times faster** than computing everything.\n",
+    "\n",
+    "2. **Carving is often much cheaper in practice** — most hexes already flow downhill correctly from master projection, so the carve loop exits early.\n",
+    "\n",
+    "To make it truly O(n log n), you'd need Dijkstra-style priority queue carving rather than per-hex traces. But for projected regions where elevations are mostly correct, this should be fast enough."
+   ]
+  },
+  {
+   "cell_type": "markdown",
+   "id": "966f375a",
+   "metadata": {},
+   "source": [
+    "Is there a way to guarentee that things connect.\n",
+    "yoo wrote ```\n",
+    "The issue is that _watershed_from_set assumes all hexes connect to the mouth, but after projection some hexes may be disconnected or the mouth may not be the absolute lowest hex. Multiple hexes end up with parent_id=None, causing the MultipleRootError.\n",
+    "\n",
+    "Fix: use BFS from mouth, only adding hexes that actually drain to an already-added hex:\n",
+    "```\n",
+    "the point of the carve to get everthing going to a single spot\n",
+    "\n",
+    "##### 🤖Reply🤖<!-- SOLVEIT_SEPARATOR_7f3a9b2c -->\n",
+    "\n",
+    "🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠\n",
+    "\n",
+    "The problem is `terrain.lowest_neighbor(idx)` returns the lowest neighbor **globally**, not within `hex_set`. So even after carving, if a hex's lowest neighbor is outside the set, `parent_id` is `None`.\n",
+    "\n",
+    "Fix by filtering to only consider neighbors in the set:\n",
+    "\n",
+    "```python\n",
+    "@classmethod\n",
+    "def _watershed_from_set(cls, terrain: Terrain, hex_set: set[int], \n",
+    "                        mouth_idx: int, style: StyleCSS) -> Watershed:\n",
+    "    \"\"\"Build single Watershed from hex set by carving to mouth.\"\"\"\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    # Step 1: Carve paths to mouth\n",
+    "    for idx in hex_set:\n",
+    "        if idx == mouth_idx:\n",
+    "            continue\n",
+    "        cls._carve_to_target(terrain, idx, mouth_idx, hex_set)\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    # Step 2: Build river - sort ascending by elevation (lowest first)\n",
+    "    sorted_hexes = sorted(hex_set, key=lambda i: terrain.elevations[i])\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    river = River(terrain)\n",
+    "    river.ocean_outlet = mouth_idx if terrain.elevations[mouth_idx] <= 0 else None\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    hex_to_node = {}\n",
+    "    node_id = 0\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    for idx in sorted_hexes:\n",
+    "        # Find lowest neighbor WITHIN hex_set only\n",
+    "        neighbors_in_set = [n for n in terrain.ring(idx, 1) if n in hex_set]\n",
+    "        if neighbors_in_set:\n",
+    "            downstream = min(neighbors_in_set, key=lambda n: terrain.elevations[n])\n",
+    "            # Only use as parent if it's actually lower\n",
+    "            if terrain.elevations[downstream] < terrain.elevations[idx]:\n",
+    "                parent_id = hex_to_node.get(downstream)\n",
+    "            else:\n",
+    "                parent_id = None  # This is a local minimum (mouth)\n",
+    "        else:\n",
+    "            parent_id = None\n",
+    "        \n",
+    "        river.tree.create_node(tag=\"segment\", identifier=node_id,\n",
+    "                               parent=parent_id, data=[idx])\n",
+    "        hex_to_node[idx] = node_id\n",
+    "        river.hexes.add(idx)\n",
+    "        node_id += 1\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    region = HexRegion(hexes=hex_set, hexGrid=terrain.hexGrid)\n",
+    "    return Watershed(region=region, tributary=river, style=style)\n",
+    "```\n",
+    "\n",
+    "This still could have multiple roots if the carving didn't fully connect everything. If you need **guaranteed single root**, combine with the BFS approach:\n",
+    "\n",
+    "```python\n",
+    "@classmethod\n",
+    "def _watershed_from_set(cls, terrain: Terrain, hex_set: set[int], \n",
+    "                        mouth_idx: int, style: StyleCSS) -> Watershed:\n",
+    "    # Step 1: Carve\n",
+    "    for idx in hex_set:\n",
+    "        if idx != mouth_idx:\n",
+    "            cls._carve_to_target(terrain, idx, mouth_idx, hex_set)\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    # Step 2: BFS from mouth - guarantees single root\n",
+    "    river = River(terrain)\n",
+    "    river.ocean_outlet = mouth_idx if terrain.elevations[mouth_idx] <= 0 else None\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    hex_to_node = {mouth_idx: 0}\n",
+    "    river.tree.create_node(tag=\"segment\", identifier=0, parent=None, data=[mouth_idx])\n",
+    "    river.hexes.add(mouth_idx)\n",
+    "    node_id = 1\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    queue = [mouth_idx]\n",
+    "    while queue:\n",
+    "        current = queue.pop(0)\n",
+    "        for neighbor in terrain.ring(current, 1):\n",
+    "            if neighbor not in hex_set or neighbor in hex_to_node:\n",
+    "                continue\n",
+    "            # Does neighbor drain to current?\n",
+    "            neighbors_in_set = [n for n in terrain.ring(neighbor, 1) if n in hex_set]\n",
+    "            lowest = min(neighbors_in_set, key=lambda n: terrain.elevations[n])\n",
+    "            if lowest == current:\n",
+    "                river.tree.create_node(tag=\"segment\", identifier=node_id,\n",
+    "                                      parent=hex_to_node[current], data=[neighbor])\n",
+    "                hex_to_node[neighbor] = node_id\n",
+    "                river.hexes.add(neighbor)\n",
+    "                node_id += 1\n",
+    "                queue.append(neighbor)\n",
+    "    \n",
+    "    region = HexRegion(hexes=hex_set, hexGrid=terrain.hexGrid)\n",
+    "    return Watershed(region=region, tributary=river, style=style)\n",
+    "```\n",
+    "\n",
+    "The BFS version guarantees connectivity but may leave some hexes out of the river tree (they'll still be in `region.hexes`)."
+   ]
+  },
